Offense/Air/Ranged at 85-95 ensures concentrated attacks on Delhi and their core cities. UseNuke at 80 signals willingness to deploy nuclear weapons if India advances. Gold/Happiness at 80-90 sustains unit maintenance and prevents combat penalties. Espionage at 100 directs spies to sabotage India's spaceship production in Constantinople and Calcutta. Mobilization at 30 begins reinforcing depleted units without overtaxing economy.

- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Montezuma, leader of The Aztecs (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Aztecs
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Jaguar, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Unit: Otomi, Replacing Warrior (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Telpochcalli, Replacing Library (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Huey Teocalli, Replacing Grand Temple (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Civilization Ability: Killing enemy Units grants 150% of their Strength as Gold and Faith. When you win a war (War Score of 25+), a Golden Age begins.
      - Leader: Montezuma
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      
      ## Policies
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 8
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 8

        - HostileBias: 8

        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 3
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 8
        - MinorCivWarBias: 5
        - DenounceWillingness: 9
        - Forgiveness: 5
        - Meanness: 5
        - Neediness: 5
        - Chattiness: 2
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Reduce Meanness slightly to leave room for potential diplomatic deals with other civs (e.g., trade routes, open borders) while maintaining overall aggressive posture toward India and Zulus.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: New World Order (Policy)
        - Rationale: New World Order will give immediate -2 Unhappiness (critical with current 38% happiness and combat penalty) and boost production and culture from Police Stations/Constabularies, helping to finish gold‑generating buildings faster.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Korea
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Korea has capitulated and become our vassal, adopting Catholicism across their cities. Previous maximum hostility modifiers (-100) are now counterproductive. Resetting to 0 allows natural vassal-master relationship bonuses to function, enabling continued tribute, trade routes, and strategic resource access crucial for the Egyptian war effort.
        - UpdatedTurn: 315
        
        ### The Zulus
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Zulu war is progressing excellently (66% war score, 30% war weariness vs their 54%). Their capital Ulundi is within reach and capturing it would give us a third capital (33% domination progress). Max hostility ensures AI commits sufficient forces to finish this war quickly, freeing up 3,000+ military strength for the American front where we're under naval attack.
        - UpdatedTurn: 403
        
        ### The Huns
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: The Huns have been defeated and eliminated from the game. Resetting our maximum hostility modifiers from -100/-100 to neutral 0/0 to reflect their removal from the strategic map and reduce ongoing warmonger penalties associated with a non-existent civilization.
        - UpdatedTurn: 408
        
        ### Byzantium
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Byzantium has been defeated and no longer exists as a relevant player in this game. Resetting relationship modifiers from -100/-100 to neutral 0/0 to reflect their elimination and reduce unnecessary diplomatic penalties.
        - UpdatedTurn: 408
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Egypt is militarily crushed (665 strength) and offers no economic benefit. Our -100 hostility modifiers generate unnecessary warmonger penalties that hurt our World Congress standing (we lead with 16 delegates). Resetting to neutral reduces penalties while maintaining the option to capture their wonders (Louvre, Nalanda) later through India proxy wars. Egypt provides zero GPT relief, so diplomatic normalization costs us nothing while improving our global image before the India confrontation.
        - UpdatedTurn: 416
        
        ### America
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: America war is concluding with Chicago as final target. Maximum relations ensure immediate peace acceptance once captured, ending 200+ GPT war costs. With New York already secured, Chicago's fall will trigger capitulation, freeing resources for India confrontation in 8 turns. This is pure economic triage to prevent bankruptcy.
        - UpdatedTurn: 418
        
        ### India
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: India remains the existential threat to all victory paths: 62% domination vs our 37%, 4 culturally influential civs vs our 1 (and 1 turn from influencing us), Apollo Program built vs our none, and 15,340 military strength with Giant Death Robots vs our 20,047. Peace treaty expires in 2 turns - maximum hostility ensures immediate declaration and total war commitment. We cannot afford half-measures; economic crisis be damned, military victory is the only path. Their Delhi capture would instantly give us domination parity and massive gold/plunder to solve our -1222 GPT crisis.
        - UpdatedTurn: 421
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: With a catastrophic –1 021 GPT and only 379 gold in the treasury, immediate economic triage is required. Maximizing Gold (100), Happiness (100), Infrastructure (100) and Tile Improvement (100) will accelerate the construction of gold‑generating buildings, trade routes and city connections. Simultaneously boosting Diplomacy (100) and Espionage (100) helps protect our remaining city‑state allies after the recent –3 000 influence loss in Bunkeya and Zurich, and may secure extra GPT from deals. Keeping Offense high (70) and Mobilization at zero ensures we focus on war preparation without adding new unit maintenance costs. Growth (80) ensures cities work more gold‑producing tiles. This balance keeps us primed for the decisive war with India while stemming the economic bleed.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        
        ### Flavors

        - Defense: 45
        - Mobilization: 0
        - CityDefense: 40
        - MilitaryTraining: 0
        - Recon: 0

            - EnactedOn: -1

Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - American Sphere of Influence: Kuala Lumpur:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: International Space Station:
            - Description: Begins the International Space Station project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : International Space Station wonder appears in Capital. (+1 Production from Scientists, and +1 Science from Engineers. Great Scientists provide 33% more Science when used to discover new Technology. +100 in City where it is built when you unlock a new Technology, scaling with Era.)
              : Free Great Scientist.
              : One time Research boost.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Rocketry.
            - Proposer: India
          - Enact: Decolonization:
            - Description: Enforce global decolonization. If successful, the Influence of all Players with all City-States is lowered to 50, unless they are permanent allies.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: Egypt
          - Enact: Aztec Sphere of Influence: Lhasa:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: The Aztecs
        - Contender: The Aztecs

        You, Montezuma (leader of The Aztecs, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 422.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Montezuma, leader of The Aztecs (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Aztecs
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Jaguar, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Unit: Otomi, Replacing Warrior (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Telpochcalli, Replacing Library (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Huey Teocalli, Replacing Grand Temple (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Civilization Ability: Killing enemy Units grants 150% of their Strength as Gold and Faith. When you win a war (War Score of 25+), a Golden Age begins.
      - Leader: Montezuma
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      
      ## Policies
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.

            - Diplomatic: We have 18 delegates (45%), need 40. Voting in 5 turns.

            - America: Peace treaty has 8 turns remaining. They capitulated and are our vassal.
            - Korea: Vassal, friendly.
            - Zulus: War ongoing? Wait, let me check. In Players section, Zulus show "Denounced Them" for us. But in our opinion section we say "Our real approach is Hostile" and we have -100/-100. Looking at Victory Progress, Zulus still exist with 6 cities and 1367 military strength. But wait, our strategies say we have a war with Zulus progressing excellently. However, looking at the tactical zones, we have friendly strength around Ulundi (6388 vs 1027 Zulu). Actually I don't see active war status in the relationships - it just says "Denounced Them". But our previous strategy mentions 66% war score. This might be outdated or the peace happened?
              
            Wait, looking at India's relationships: India has Zulus as "Our Vassal (Voluntary)". Wait, Zulus are India's vassal? Yes! In Player 1 (India) relationships: "The Zulus: Distance: Close, Our Vassal (Voluntary), Open Borders". So Zulus capitulated to India! That explains a lot.
            
            Wait, but our strategy says "Zulu war is progressing excellently". But in the latest turn events, we see Zulu units moving around, and our citadel captured some Zulu tiles (events 152-155). Also at Turn 421 event 0: "SpyOwner: 3: Egypt" spying on Ulundi. 
            
            Actually, looking at war scores: India vs Egypt war score 100%. But anyway, the Zulus being India's vassal is critical information I missed in the strategy section which seems outdated.
            
            **Peace Treaties:**
            - India: Peace treaty expired at turn 421/422 (remaining: 0). We are free to declare war on India now.
            - America: Peace treaty has 8 turns remaining. They are our vassal (capitulated) - wait, in Player 7 (America), it says "Our Master (Capitulated)". So America is our vassal! We don't need to capture Chicago necessarily for domination since Washington is already our vassal. Actually we DO control Washington? Let me check our capitals: Tenochtitlan, Seoul, Washington. Yes, we have Washington! America is our vassal.
            
            Wait, but in Cities, Washington has Owner: The Aztecs, IsOccupied: true. Chicago and New York are puppets? Wait, New York and Chicago show IsPuppet: true, with 0 production and resistance turns. So we must have captured them recently. But America is listed as "Our Vassal (Capitulated)". So they capitulated but we still took New York and Chicago? Or did we capture them and then they capitulated?
            
            Actually, looking at diplomatic deals: America gives us Vassalage and 317 Gold, we give peace. That was on turn 418 (8 turns remaining, now 421 means 5 turns passed? Wait UpdatedTurn 418 with TurnsRemaining 8. Now it's turn 422, so 4 turns passed, 4 turns remaining. But the event log shows PlayerDoneTurn at turn 422 us, so maybe the turn counter advanced and it's now between 422-423. Anyway, the peace treaty with America still has some turns.
            
            **Critical Issue: Economy**
            - -909 GPT! Only 55 gold.
            - We need to fix this immediately or we'll go bankrupt and lose units.
            - We have a LOT of military units (23080 strength, 65 units) but supply is 173. We're well under supply cap, so unit count isn't the issue. But maintenance is killing us.
            - World Congress has Global Peace Accords active? No, it shows EnactedOn: -1. Wait, Spaceflight Regulations is active (enacted 416). International Space Station proposal by India.
            
            **India Threat:**
            - India has 15868 military strength (was 15340 in strategy, now higher). We have 23080, so we actually outnumber them significantly!
            - India is building spaceship parts (3/6 done, 50%). They will win science victory if unchecked.
            - India has 30 cities, 1079 population. Much larger empire.
            - Gandhi is unhappy (48% happiness).
            - Their ideology is Order, ours is Autocracy.
            
            **War with India - Immediate Priority:**
            The peace treaty expired. We MUST declare war immediately. With 23080 vs 15868 military strength, we have a significant advantage. We need to push for Delhi and other capitals. India has 5 capitals: Delhi, Thebes, Attila's Court, Ulundi, Constantinople. If we capture Delhi and maybe others, we could get domination parity.
            
            But wait, can we afford a war with -909 GPT? War will make it worse (unit maintenance, no trade). However, our unique ability gives us gold and faith from kills, and golden ages from war wins. Plus we can pillage and capture cities for gold.
            
            **City States:**
            - We are allied with Bunkeya (674 influence), Sogut (406), Lhasa (384), Zurich (643).
            - Sidon is allied with America (38) but we have 287 influence friends.
            - Kuala Lumpur allied with America (501) but we have 311 influence friends.
            - India is actively courting our city states. Their Great Diplomat went to Sogut.
            
            **World Congress:**
            - Voting in 5 turns.
            - India proposed International Space Station.
            - Egypt proposed Decolonization (would hurt India/US maybe).
            - We proposed Aztec Sphere of Influence: Lhasa.
            
            **Diplomatic Victory:**
            - Need 40 votes, have 18. Almost impossible to reach in time without total city-state control, which India is competing for.
            
            **Science Victory:**
            - We're researching Stealth (9 turns). We don't have Apollo Program. Realistically can't win science.
            
            **Cultural Victory:**
            - India is influential over us. We are not close to influencing India. Can't win culture.
            
            **Domination Victory:**
            - Only realistic path. We need 5 more capitals. India has 5 capitals in their empire. If we capture Delhi, Thebes, Attila's Court, Constantinople, and Ulundi, we get all 8. But Zulu cities... wait, does India control Ulundi? Let me check.
            
            In Cities section under India: 
            - Attila's Court (occupied)
            - Constantinople (occupied) 
            - Thebes (puppet)
            - Ulundi - Wait, I don't see Ulundi listed under India's cities! Let me check. Looking through the list... Delhi, Mumbai, Vijayanagara, Pataliputra, Varanasi, Agra, Calcutta, Lahore, Bangalore, Hyderabad, Madurai, Spokane, Attila's Court, Ohrid, Kyiv, Constantinople, Adrianople, Nicaea, Assur, Antioch, Varna, Alexandria, Luba, Memphis, Heliopolis, Thebes, Ahmedabad, Elephantine, Kolhapur, Giza. No Ulundi!
            
            So India does NOT have Ulundi. The Zulus still exist with Ulundi. But India has Zulus as vassal. So Zulus are a voluntary vassal of India, meaning India doesn't control their capital for domination purposes, but can summon them to war. So capturing Zulu cities doesn't help India's domination count directly.
            
            Wait, our current capitals: Tenochtitlan, Seoul, Washington. That's 3.
            India's capitals: Delhi, Thebes, Attila's Court, Constantinople. That's 4 in the city list, but victory progress says India has 5: Delhi, Thebes, Attila's Court, Ulundi, Constantinople. Wait, the game says India controls Ulundi as a capital? But Ulundi is not in India's city list. Unless... the Zulus capitulated, so maybe the game counts vassal capitals? No, vassals retain their capitals. Let me re-read the victory progress.
            
            "India: CapitalsControlled: Delhi, Thebes, Attila's Court, Ulundi, Constantinople"
            But Ulundi is not owned by India in the city list. This might mean the game counts capitals of vassals as controlled by the master for domination purposes? Actually, in Civ V vox populi, if you vassalize a civ, do you control their capital? The description says "Control or vassalize all original capitals." Oh! "Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence." But for the master, a vassal's capital counts as controlled! Ah yes, that's standard in Vox Populi. So India controls Ulundi through the Zulu vassalage. Similarly, we control Seoul through Korea and Washington through America.
            
            So India effectively has 5 capitals, we have 3. If we capture Delhi, we reduce them to 4 and increase to 4. But we also need to control 8 total. Wait, there were originally 8 major civs? Let's count: Korea, India, The Aztecs, Egypt, The Huns, The Zulus, Byzantium, America. That's 8. The Huns and Byzantium are defeated. Their capitals (Attila's Court, Constantinople) are now owned by India. 
            
            For us to win domination, we need all 8 capitals either directly controlled or vassalized. Currently:
            - Tenochtitlan: us (direct)
            - Seoul: Korea (our vassal) - counts for us
            - Washington: America (our vassal) - counts for us
            - Delhi: India (enemy)
            - Thebes: Egypt (now owned by India as puppet)
            - Attila's Court: The Huns (extinct, owned by India)
            - Constantinople: Byzantium (extinct, owned by India)
            - Ulundi: The Zulus (India's vassal)
            
            So we need to get Delhi, Thebes/Attila's/Constantinople (India has these), and break Zulu vassalage to get Ulundi or capture it ourselves.
            
            But if we conquer Delhi, we get one. If we can make Egypt our vassal or capture Thebes? But Thebes is India's puppet. Attila's Court and Constantinople are also India's. India has 4 direct capitals (Delhi, Thebes, Attila's Court, Constantinople) plus 1 vassal capital (Ulundi).
            
            If India captured Thebes from Egypt, Thebes is still Egypt's original capital. So if we capture Thebes from India, it counts as controlling Egypt's capital. Same for Attila's Court and Constantinople.
            
            So our path: capture Delhi (India's capital), Thebes, Attila's Court, Constantinople from India, and either force Zulus to become our vassal or capture Ulundi. That's 5 capitals to acquire.
            
            Given our military advantage (23080 vs 15868) and India's unhappiness (48%), we should attack NOW.
            
            **Economic Crisis:**
            - -909 GPT is unsustainable. But we're at war readiness. Actually, war with India will cost more in unit maintenance, but we'll gain plunder and pillage gold.
            - India has 4541 gold and +52 GPT.
            - We need to capture rich cities fast.
            - We have 18 delegates and can maybe pass something useful, but our focus must be war.
            
            **Strategy Decisions:**
            
            1. **Flavors/Grand Strategy:** Must be Conquest. Need to prioritize Offense, but also consider our economic collapse. We need to end wars quickly and profit from them. We should keep Mobilization moderate (we're already producing well), but focus on war execution. Since we have overwhelming air power (heavy bombers, zeros, jet fighters), we should leverage that. 
               - However, -909 GPT means every unit hurts. We might want to actually DISBAND some unnecessary units? But we can't directly control that. We can set flavors to reduce production and focus on existing units.
               - Actually, our cities are building useful things. We should not build more units unless necessary; we should use existing forces.
               - Set Offense to 100 to ensure aggressive war posture. Set Air to 100 since air dominance is key. Nuke/UseNuke should be very high to break India's defenses. India's defense strength in Delhi is 118, but we have nukes presumably? Wait, we have 12 Uranium. We can build nukes. Actually we may already have them - UseNuke is 50 in previous strategy.
               - Gold flavor should still be high to address GPT, but war takes priority.
               - We should reduce Happiness since we're already building it and need war focus. Actually happiness is at 42%, we need it.
            
            Wait, looking at our cities, many are working on Broadcast Towers, Public Works, Museums, etc. We need to finish wars, not infrastructure. But with flavors, the AI decides new production after current queues.
            
            Let's think about relationship with India: it's already -100/-100. Good.
            
            **Research:**
            - Currently researching Stealth (9 turns). What should be next?
            - Options only show Refrigeration as available. That's not very useful right now. But we might want something for war... Actually the options section under Technologies only lists Refrigeration. The tool accepts any technology name though - but the instruction says "must only use options from the # Options section". So I can only set research to Refrigeration (or None). Refrigeration allows Work Boats to construct Offshore Platform and increases trade route distance, unlocks Electronics, Computers. Not helpful for war. I'd rather not set next research, or set None.
            
            Wait, the instruction says: "For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section". The technology option listed is Refrigeration. So I should either set Refrigeration or not use the tool. Actually I could also set None. But Refrigeration seems poor. I'll skip set-research or set None.
            
            **Policy:**
            - NextPolicyTurns: 6
            - Currently set to New World Order (Policy) in our strategy.
            - But wait, NWO is a Level 1 tenet of Autocracy. Do we already have Autocracy? Yes, we have several Autocracy policies: Elite Forces, Military-Industrial Complex, United Front, Air Supremacy, Third Alternative, Martial Spirit. We might already have NWO? Let me check.
            - Looking at our policy branches, we don't have "New World Order" listed specifically. The listed ones under Autocracy are: Elite Forces, Military-Industrial Complex, United Front, Air Supremacy, Third Alternative, Martial Spirit. That's 6 tenets. So we don't have NWO yet. It would be our 7th tenet.
            - NWO gives -2 unhappiness from distress, Police Stations and Constabularies +3 Culture +5 Production, built 100% faster. This is good for our happiness and production.
            - The other Autocracy tenets available in options: Lebensraum, Futurism, Iron Fist.
            - Lebensraum: Culture and GA points on border expansion, Citadels in foreign territory, retain tiles after razing. Useful for war.
            - Futurism: Palace tourism boost, +2 Culture from Great Works, 50 Tourism when conquering a city. Also good.
            - Iron Fist: Vassals can't be liberated, capitulated vassals can't rebel, +25% yields from city-states/vassals, +50% worker rate. We already have vassals, so useful.
            
            Actually, for our policy next, NWO is still a solid choice, but given we're about to go to total war with India, Lebensraum or Futurism or Iron Fist might be better. Iron Fist secures our vassals (America, Korea) and increases yields. Lebensraum gives us citadel-forwarding ability which is great for the India war. Futurism gives tourism but that's not our path.
            
            Wait, the peace treaty with America has 8 turns left. They are our vassal. Can vassals rebel? In Vox Populi, yes, if war score is low and they build strength. Iron Fist would prevent that. But we have more pressing issues.
            
            Our previous rationale for NWO was economic triage. But we're going to war now. Is NWO still the best choice?
            - We have 30 cities, many with Constabularies already. The -2 unhappiness is empire-wide, so -60 unhappiness? No, "-2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities" - that's huge. Our unhappiness is at 42% (meaning -58% happiness? Actually HappinessPercentage is 42, which is quite unhappy).
            - The production boost to Police Stations/Constabularies is nice but we need immediate impact.
            
            However, we have 6 turns until next policy anyway, so by the time we get it, the war will be ongoing.
            
            But looking at available non-Autocracy policies, we could also pick a Rationalism policy (Scientific Revolution or Enlightenment) or Industry (Division of Labor, Free Trade). But war demands Autocracy tenets.
            
            Actually, I think NWO is still fine given our catastrophic happiness. But let me reconsider: the policy we set "next" will be adopted when we accumulate enough culture, in 6 turns. The war will just be starting. NWO will help our economy and happiness during the war. I'll keep NWO.
            
            **Relationships:**
            - India: Already -100/-100. Good.
            - Zulus: We had -100/-100 but Zulus are now India's vassal. Should we reset? If we're at war with India, India might summon Zulus. We should keep hostility toward Zulus or reset? Actually, since Zulus are India's vassal, they will fight alongside India regardless. Our hostility doesn't matter functionally if India calls them. But we might want to avoid a two-front war. However, Zulus are weak (1367 strength) and our border with them has huge friendly strength. I think keep -100/-100 or reduce it. Actually, we might want to make peace with Zulus if possible, but since they're a vassal of India, they can't make peace independently. Wait, in Civ V vox populi, can a vassal make peace? Usually no, the master controls peace. So we're stuck fighting Zulus if India calls them. Our hostility setting doesn't matter much, but let's keep it to ensure AI doesn't try to be friendly with a vassal of our enemy.
            
            Wait, looking at our diplomatic relations in the game: The Zulus: "Denounced Them". No war? In military zones, we see Zulu units in Ulundi and we have friendly zone around it with 6388 strength. We might already be at war? The game doesn't explicitly list "War" in the relationships summary for Zulus. It only says "Denounced Them". But our previous strategy said war score 66%. This is confusing. But it doesn't matter much since we're about to fight India and Zulus will likely join.
            
            Actually, critical realization: If Zulus are India's vassal, and we declare war on India, India CAN force Zulus to join. So we'll be fighting Zulus anyway. Good thing we have troops near Ulundi.
            
            **America:**
            - They are our vassal, peace treaty for 8 more turns. We have +100/+100. This is good, keeps them docile. They have almost no military (458 strength).
            
            **Egypt:**
            - Military crushed (185 strength). We have neutral relations. Should we ally with Egypt against India? Egypt is at war with India (India's war score 100%). Egypt voted for Decolonization. They hate India. We could set relationship with Egypt to friendly to get them to join our war against India? But Egypt is irrelevant with 185 strength. And we denounced them. Setting to friendly might be useful to avoid backstabs, but they pose no threat. Let's leave neutral.
            
            **Korea:**
            - Our vassal, friendly. Good.
            
            **Critical Actions Needed:**
            
            1. **set-flavors**: Prepare for total war against India. We need:
               - GrandStrategy: Conquest
               - Offense: Maximum or near maximum (100)
               - Air: Very high (air superiority crucial) (100)
               - Nuke: Very high (100)
               - UseNuke: High (100 or 80) - using nukes on Delhi could break their defense
               - Mobilization: Increase to build replacement units (but we have low gold... hmm). Actually Mobilization at 0 was to save costs. But we're going to war and will need replacements. Let's set to 50 or 70.
               - CityDefense: Lower (we're attacking), maybe 20
               - Gold: Still important, maybe 80 instead of 100
               - Happiness: Very important, 100 (war causes happiness issues)
               - Science: Lower, 40 (we're not winning science)
               - Culture: Lower, 30
               - Production: 80 (need to build war materiel)
               - Infrastructure/TileImprovement: Drop significantly, we're done building infrastructure for now. Set to 20 or 0.
               - Diplomacy: Drop, we're at war. City-states are less important than survival. But we should keep some to maintain allies. Maybe 40.
               - Espionage: Keep moderate to protect our cities and steal from India.
            
            Wait, we have so many flavors at 100 currently. We need to rebalance for war execution.
            
            Current flavors:
            - Gold: 100
            - Happiness: 100
            - Infrastructure: 100
            - TileImprovement: 100
            - Diplomacy: 100
            - Espionage: 100
            - Offense: 70
            - Nuke: 95
            - Air: 85
            - Science: 85
            - Production: 90
            - Ranged: 75
            - Growth: 80
            - Mobile: 55
            - Antiair: 55
            - UseNuke: 50
            
            This is too spread out. For pure war against India with our economic constraints, we need:
            - Offense: 100 (max attack)
            - Air: 100 (we have massive air force, use it)
            - Nuke: 100 (break their strongpoints)
            - UseNuke: 100 (actually use them)
            - Gold: 80 (still need to manage economy, but war will bring plunder)
            - Happiness: 80 (unhappy hurts combat)
            - Mobilization: 60 (build some replacements)
            - CityDefense: 30 (our homeland is secure)
            - Science: 40
            - Culture: 20
            - Infrastructure: 10
            - TileImprovement: 10
